## 💬 Response

I now have confirmed national data through Week 26 (=4.61). The critical gap is the Week 27 national provisional value. The Saga page confirms national W26=4.61 but the W27 national value isn't published on the sources checked yet (Saga reports national with 1-week lag; its W27 page still shows national W26). Let me try the Hokkaido page (which has a HFMD row through W27) and the JIHS provisional table directly to obtain the national W27 figure.

## 💬 Response

I have gathered comprehensive data. The national Week 27 provisional value has not yet been published by JIHS as of the accessible sources (data publishes ~1 week after target week; the W27 provisional national table would publish around July 10-15, 2026). All aggregators checked (Saga, Nishinomiya, beans-mc) still cite national W26=4.61 as the latest national figure, with W27 available only at prefecture level. ## Research Report: Japan HFMD (手足口病) National Sentinel Surveillance — 2026 Season, Weeks 20–27

### Answer to the primary question (Week 27, 2026)

**As of the accessible data (retrieved 2026-07-14), the national (全国) provisional per-sentinel value (定点当たり報告数) for hand-foot-and-mouth disease for Week 27 (June 29–July 5, 2026) has NOT yet been published by JIHS.** The JIHS IDWR provisional (速報) index for 2026 confirms that **Week 26 is the most recent week available**. This is consistent with the stated ~1-week publication lag; the national Week 27 provisional figure is expected to appear on the JIHS provisional table around July 10–15, 2026.

All aggregator sources checked (Saga Prefecture / H-CRISIS, Nishinomiya City, beans-mc, jmedj) still cite **national W26 = 4.61** as the latest national figure. Week 27 data is currently available only at the **prefecture/local level** (e.g., Saga W27 = 11.83, Hyogo prefecture-level W27 = 6.66, Hokkaido W27 = 0.68), not nationally.

### Confirmed national per-sentinel values (定点当たり報告数, 全国)

| Week | Period (2026) | National per-sentinel | Status | Source |
|------|---------------|----------------------|--------|--------|
| W20 | May 11–17 | (increase began; exact value not retrieved) | Confirmed rise start | jmedj/JIHS |
| W23 | Jun 1–7 | **1.98** | Confirmed | jmedj (JIHS IDWR) |
| W24 | Jun 8–14 | **2.75** | Confirmed | beans-mc (JIHS) |
| W25 | Jun 15–21 | **3.40** | Confirmed | jmedj + beans-mc (JIHS) |
| W26 | Jun 22–28 | **4.61** | Confirmed (10,396 cases / ~2,350 pediatric sentinels) | Saga/H-CRISIS (JIHS) |
| W27 | Jun 29–Jul 5 | **Not yet published nationally** | Pending | JIHS provisional index |

### Week-over-week trajectory (national, 2026 season)

- The national HFMD per-sentinel count has **increased continuously since Week 20** (病原体: multiple sources explicitly state "第20週以降増加が続いている" — "increasing since Week 20").
- Confirmed sequence: **W23 = 1.98 → W24 = 2.75 (+38.9%) → W25 = 3.40 (+23.6%) → W26 = 4.61 (+35.6%)**.
- W25 was **4.59× the 10-year median** for the same week (10-yr median ≈ 0.74).
- Geographic pattern: the wave began in Kyushu (九州), spread through the Chugoku region (中国地方) into all of western Japan by W24, and reached the Tokyo metropolitan area (首都圏) by W25–W26. Tokyo entered "警報レベル" (warning level) in Week 26.
- Prefecture-level W24 leaders: Oita 15.89, Shimane 14.36, Kagoshima 14.06, Kumamoto 10.3. As of W24, 9 prefectures were at warning level (≥ threshold of "警報レベル"); 37 prefectures showed week-over-week increases.

### Data vintage / methodology notes
- Metric: 定点当たり報告数 = cases per pediatric sentinel site (~2,350–3,000 小児科定点医療機関 nationally).
- Warning-level (警報) threshold for HFMD: 5.0 per sentinel. National W26 (4.61) is approaching but below this; many individual prefectures already exceed it.
- Provisional (速報) values are subject to minor upward revision in the finalized (確定) IDWR report.

Now I'll record the structured findings.

## 💬 Response

## Summary

**Primary finding on Week 27:** The national (全国) provisional per-sentinel value for HFMD (手足口病) for **Week 27 (June 29–July 5, 2026) has NOT yet been published by JIHS** as of the accessible data (retrieved 2026-07-14). The JIHS IDWR provisional index confirms **Week 26 is the most recent national week available**, consistent with the ~1-week publication lag. Week 27 national data should appear around July 10–15, 2026. Currently W27 exists only at prefecture/local level (Saga 11.83, Hyogo 6.66, Hokkaido 0.68).

**Confirmed national per-sentinel values:**
- **Week 25** (Jun 15–21) = **3.40** ✓ (confirmed by both jmedj/JIHS and beans-mc)
- **Week 26** (Jun 22–28) = **4.61** ✓ (10,396 cases / ~2,350 pediatric sentinels)

**Trajectory (national, weeks 20–27):** Continuous increase every week since Week 20. Confirmed sequence: W23 = 1.98 → W24 = 2.75 (+38.9%) → W25 = 3.40 (+23.6%) → W26 = 4.61 (+35.6%). W25 ran ~4.59× the 10-year median. The wave moved west→east: Kyushu → Chugoku/western Japan (by W24) → Tokyo metro area (by W25–26, Tokyo hit warning level at W26). The national warning threshold is 5.0 per sentinel — national W26 (4.61) is approaching but below it, while many individual prefectures (Oita, Shimane, Kagoshima, Kumamoto, etc.) already far exceed it.

The full report with all quantitative data points and sources has been recorded via the structured output.

I now have solid, consistent data. Key finding: **national W26 = 4.61** (confirmed by multiple JIHS-citing sources: Saga H-CRISIS, beans-mc). The question description's "5.79" for W26 is an error — the actual national series is:

- W23 = 1.98, W24 = 2.75, W25 = 3.40, W26 = 4.61
- WoW ratios: ×1.389, ×1.236, ×1.356 (mean ≈ ×1.33)

Prefecture W27 data shows continued strong growth (Saga 9.42→11.83, Ehime 2.81→8.71, Hokkaido 0.32→0.68, Kanagawa 9.09, Osaka 5.96, Chiba W26=6.26). Let me run a Monte Carlo.

## 🔧 Tool: mcp__sandbox__execute_code


```json
{
  "code": "\nimport numpy as np\nnp.random.seed(42)\n\nN = 200000\n\n# National series (provisional/confirmed): W23=1.98, W24=2.75, W25=3.40, W26=4.61\n# WoW ratios\nr = np.array([2.75/1.98, 3.40/2.75, 4.61/3.40])\nlogr = np.log(r)\nprint(\"WoW ratios:\", r, \"mean logr:\", logr.mean(), \"std:\", logr.std(ddof=1))\n\n# W26 base: provisional 4.61; confirmed tends slightly higher. Model uncertainty.\nW26 = np.random.normal(4.70, 0.15, N)  # center a touch above provisional for confirmed\n\n# W27 log-ratio: center ~0.30 (x1.35), std widened for accel/decel model uncertainty\n# Season peaks W28-30, so W27 still rising strongly; prefecture data supports continued growth\nlogratio = np.random.normal(0.30, 0.15, N)\n\n# small extra upside tail: eastern surge into big population centers (Tokyo/Kanagawa/Chiba)\n# add occasional acceleration bump\naccel = np.random.normal(0.0, 0.06, N)\nW27 = W26 * np.exp(logratio + accel)\n\npcts = [1,5,10,20,25,30,40,50,60,70,75,80,90,95,99]\nvals = np.percentile(W27, pcts)\nfor p,v in zip(pcts, vals):\n    print(f\"P{p}: {v:.2f}\")\nprint(\"mean:\", W27.mean())\nprint(\"P(>5):\", (W27>5).mean(), \"P(>8):\", (W27>8).mean(), \"P(<4):\", (W27<4).mean())\n"
}
```

```json
}
```

## 🔧 Tool: mcp__sandbox__execute_code

```json
{
  "code": "\nimport numpy as np\nnp.random.seed(2026)\nN = 400000\n\n# Main model: national W26=4.61 (well-sourced from JIHS). Confirmed slightly higher.\nW26 = np.random.normal(4.70, 0.15, N)\nu = np.random.random(N)\nlogratio = np.empty(N)\nbase = u < 0.58; up = (u>=0.58)&(u<0.85); dec = u>=0.85\nlogratio[base] = np.random.normal(0.29, 0.11, base.sum())\nlogratio[up]   = np.random.normal(0.43, 0.12, up.sum())\nlogratio[dec]  = np.random.normal(0.16, 0.09, dec.sum())\nW27_main = W26 * np.exp(logratio)\n\n# Hedge model (15%): resolver figure on a higher scale (~ question author's 5.79 series)\nW26h = np.random.normal(5.79, 0.20, N)\nW27_hedge = W26h * np.exp(np.random.normal(0.30, 0.13, N))\n\npick_hedge = np.random.random(N) < 0.15\nW27 = np.where(pick_hedge, W27_hedge, W27_main)\n\npcts = [1,5,10,20,25,30,40,50,60,70,75,80,90,95,99]\nvals = np.percentile(W27, pcts)\nfor p,v in zip(pcts, vals):\n    print(f\"P{p}: {v:.2f}\")\nprint(\"mean:\", round(W27.mean(),2), \"median:\", round(np.median(W27),2))\nprint(\"P(<4):\", round((W27<4).mean(),3),\"P(<5):\", round((W27<5).mean(),3),\n      \"P(>8):\", round((W27>8).mean(),3),\"P(>10):\", round((W27>10).mean(),3),\"P(>12):\",round((W27>12).mean(),4))\n"
}
```
